#6775. 36 Thoughtful Kitchen Gifts for Anyone Who Loves to Cook, Host, or Sip in Style

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Anyone can buy a candle. These gifts are way more personal.

The best kind of gift shows the person you care about that you know them. That you’ve considered their hobbies. After all, anyone can go out and buy a scented candle, but you are thinking about how they like to spend their free time and what makes them happy. So if you know a dinner party host, mixologist, home chef, or someone who loves a stylish kitchen, then this is the gift guide for you.

Below, dozens of smart and thoughtful kitchen gifts await you. The busy home cook will love an Instant Pot or multipurpose air fryer. The coffee or tea obsessive will love a matcha station or coffee grinder. Think outside the kitchen and into the dining room, too: an elegant yet budget-friendly canister set or berry basket could be just what they need to complete their tablescape.

The best thing about so many of our picks is that there’s a massive price range, so if you see something affordable your friend or family member may like, you don’t need to wait for a special occasion to get it for them. On the other hand, if the same person is celebrating a birthday or a holiday, feel free to splurge. We have everything any at-home cook would love and use.

No kitchen is complete without a good bottle opener, so why not opt for one that’s extra chic? Trade your gas station souvenir opener for this colorful beaded piece, inspired by the classic abacus tool. You can even rearrange the beads to suit your own aesthetic.

Any serious coffee drinker or amateur barista will know the importance of a good coffee bean grinder. There’s nothing quite like being able to control the coarseness of your own coffee for your morning brew. You can choose between 60 grind sizes, whether you’re using a moka pot or prepping cold brew.

If your date night dinner isn’t complete without a round of oysters, then this oyster plate needs to be added to your collection. The ridged porcelain edge looks like a more elegant version of a paper plate. And the indents in the dish are perfect for your East Coast dozen. It’s even dishwasher-safe.

This is the ideal martini glass for your most stylish friend. The color-blocked stems are thin enough for a swirl, and the set of two glasses comes in alternating colors. It even comes with stainless steel swizzle sticks, primed for stuffed olives.

Gift a set of serving utensils that’s a major upgrade from the typical silicone and plastic. The lustrous finish and curved handles are just too beautiful. It’s carved from sustainably sourced horn and natural shell, and it’ll make your salad bowl look like the star of the show.

Rocco’s full-size fridges have gone viral for every sophisticated host. But the latest launch is way more budget-friendly. This mini mini fridge is sized for desktops and nightstands, so whether you need a nighttime face mask or a midday Diet Coke break, it’s stored in style.

Everyone knows at least one matcha drinker who is serious about their habit. This is the station they’ll reach for every morning. The bowl, bamboo whisk, whisk stand, and sifter all ensure a clump-free, perfectly aerated brew. And it’s all gathered on a ceramic, non-slip tea tray.

Know a tinned fish lover? Fishwife has made sardines sexy again, and this advent calendar provides 12 days of fun surprises for every charcuterie board assembler. It even comes with a few non-edible pieces like a fish knife, key chain, and moisturizer.

Associate Shopping Editor Janae McKenzie has been using the electric kettle from Drew Barrymore’s line for years. It comes in a variety of pastel colors that brighten up your countertop and make short work of everything from loose-leaf tea to soup.

Any home chef serious about their spices needs a mortar and pestle that can keep up. It’s made from pure bronze, with an ergonomic handle that makes grinding peppercorns, cardamom, ginger, and garlic easy. It’s handmade by artisans in Moradabad, known as “The Brass City of India.”

These glasses look like they should be kept safe in a china cabinet, but they’re durable enough for everyday life. The shape of the glass was designed in collaboration with WhistlePig distillers to enhance the whiskey-drinking experience. The pontil mark on the bottom of each glass celebrates “perfect imperfection”—a mark leftover by Simon Pearce glassblowers.

A new paper towel holder is an upgrade you won’t think to do on your own, which makes it the perfect kitchen gift. It’s likely one of the most-visited spots in your kitchen. The painted beech wood and rubber feet keep it both stylish and secure.

Things get messy in the kitchen, and no one will say no to a gift of fresh dish towels. These are woven from organic cotton and stitched by a woman-owned textile company in Mumbai. They’re embroidered with the icons of Indian cooking: a fresh coconut, a tomato, and curry leaves.

Ash wood and granite stone are a match made in heaven to get the most of your ground pepper. McKenzie uses this with gourmet peppercorns, and with the easily refilled vessel and cut stone, it’s a tactile experience that elevates the simplest meal. The stone smooths over time as oils are released by the peppercorns, effectively seasoning it over time.

This is perfect for the pitmaster in your life. Sure, they've probably got a meat thermometer from the grocery store, but this upgrade is so worth it, especially for those long, slow-cooked meats. This high-tech, long-range Bluetooth thermometer will save them the headache of constantly checking their grill, so they can check its status from the comfort of the couch.

This is the potluck host’s secret weapon. No need to mess with the hassle of foil pans, chafing dishes, and Sterno burners. Just one glass-top electric warming tray can keep your appetizers hot for the whole party. It even comes with cool-touch handles for easy portability.

This is the hand wash McKenzie leaves out on her kitchen sink when guests come over. “The Moroccan mint scent is refreshing, and the lather is moisturizing, not stripping,” McKenzie says. “When the dinner party gets chaotic, this is the easiest way for guests to refresh themselves in the kitchen. The glass bottle is an extra sleek touch.”

There's a reason Drew Barrymore's slow cooker sold out within days of launching. Now, more than a year later, the hype has gone down a bit, making snatching one up a lot easier. It comes in a few delicious colors, but we're partial to the white one, which Barrymore aptly named White Icing.

Keep your favorite spices and garnishes within reach with this beautiful seasoning dish. It's about the capacity of a pinch bowl, but the two walnut wood compartments can hold salt, dried herbs, and even peppercorns and capers separately. Make seasoning food incredibly easy—with one pinch, any meal is elevated.

#6774. Misha & Puff’s First Womenswear Collection Gives Knitwear a New Point of View

The cult-favorite children’s knitwear brand is officially expanding to adults.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Women’s fashion has some of the best designs out there—full stop. That said, every now and then I see a well-dressed kid and think, “Damn, I wish I had something like that.” This is especially true when I see a little one sporting a design from none other than Misha & Puff, a brand known for its ethically crafted, handmade, vintage-inspired children’s clothes. And while my wardrobe carries a playful touch, I’m not necessarily looking for a supersized version of a child’s sweater, but rather the essence of that whimsy. Thankfully, I don’t have to look too far; Misha & Puff just launched its first-ever women’s collection—and it’s more than I could have hoped for.

“Knits are hot. Knits are sexy. The way that they drape, the way that they accentuate your neck, I really do feel like we can do a lot with a knit,” Sophia Roe told ELLE.com over the phone. “For me, I feel really beautiful when I put these clothes on.” The James Beard Award-winning chef—along with her daughter, Mizu—came together with artist Mara Hoffman behind the lens to create a campaign that captures the essence of Misha & Puff, highlighting the craftsmanship and expression of the brand’s new womenswear collection.

“Everything I do with the brand is very authentic and really about what I’m interested in...That’s how the kids’ brand started, with me having my son and then growing up,” Misha & Puff’s founder, Anna Wallack, said on a phone call. “The choice to launch women’s in a bigger way really comes from the pull that I’m feeling as a woman. For me, dressing women and dressing myself is about how we’re working these pieces together. How do I feel in these silhouettes? Who is this character today? I want to keep playing with that in a bigger way.”

Wallack went on to tell me that designing for the collection—which launched today—started with what she described as a “fiber-first” approach. “We work with a Pima cotton in Peru that worked so well for kids because it’s incredibly soft and it’s so wearable, and then to find that I want to wear that all the time for myself; I want to wear our merino wool,” she explained. “Our decision to launch women’s with a bigger collection really was a commitment to doing it instead of just making one sweater here and there.” The result? A range of expressive knits in a color palette reminiscent of the ’70s that encourage the wearer to be who she is as a woman today. With every solid- or heather-hued piece there’s another featuring an eye-catching print or embroidery detail. “I feel a little more permission to explore other ideas in the adult collection and what it means to be a women, too.”

This exploration extends into Misha & Puff’s campaign. Roe shared on a phone call that the idea of partnering with Misha & Puff stemmed from just being a fan of the brand. “I said, ‘I don’t know what we can do but I really want to do something with Anna.’ Little did I know that in the background she was working on this unbelievable adult collection,” she said. Though this isn’t the first project Roe has starred in with her daughter, she explained it was the first where she was shot by another mother, making the experience feel truly special—especially as she comes into her body 15 months postpartum. “I feel kind of hot as a mom. There are a lot of narratives out there about getting back to whatever that pre-baby thing is, and I just kind of rebuke that in every single way. I think what I am now is kind of amazing and I really do feel like Mara was able to bring that out in a way that no one else could have.”

On the vision for the campaign, Hoffman shared that the focus was “seeing how does a woman exist through a space or on the street, and how does she live in it?” Working to capture Roe in a documentarian way, the artist wanted to make sure the imagery felt authentic to both the brand and the people wearing it. “We are lucky to be able to capture little shots of that along the way.” Of course, shooting with a baby can come with its own unpredictability. Luckily, as Roe shared, working with someone like Hoffman helped her relax on set with the reminder, “This is just life.”

Happy Friday the 13th to all who celebrate, but especially Taylor Swift, who just announced her major return to the Country Music Association Awards. This year’s show is set to take place at the Bridgestone Arena in Nashville on Wednesday, November 18.

#6771. Design Experts Reveal When It’s Actually OK to Start Decorating for the Holidays

There’s no hard rule—but your HOA may have one.

Now that the Halloween candy bowls are empty, you might find thoughts of tinsel, garland, and fairy lights dancing in your head, but is it really time to trade your pumpkins for holiday trimmings? Some have strong opinions on the topic, half believing the jolly spirit of the holiday season doesn't start until after Thanksgiving, the other rushing to unbox their silver bells as soon as the clock strikes 12 on November 1—but the choice isn’t always yours to make.

If you belong to a Homeowners’ Association (and over 78.1 million Americans do), the question of when to put up your outdoor décor may be dictated by their guidelines. HOAs tend to have rules about holiday decorations, restrictions covering their location (within the boundaries of your yard, of course), sound level (not too loud), safety (no open flames), light color, and when the decorations can be installed. In some communities, you can even be fined for bringing out the twinkle lights and nativity creche before Thanksgiving—this happened to one Florida man in 2002, when he was hit with a fine of $1,000 for putting his lights up on November 6.

If you don’t live in an HOA, you may not get fined for decorating too early, but you will get judged by neighbors who find pre-Thanksgiving holiday displays a little, well, tacky. To be fair, holding until after Thanksgiving does have its perks—why would you pass on the opportunity to decorate a gorgeous fall tablescape? In the spirit of peace on earth and goodwill toward man, we asked design experts what to do. Here’s what they said.

Additional copy by Meghan Shouse and Alyssa Khan.

You might follow a religious tradition that guides your holiday decorating, like placing the Baby Jesus in the nativity on Christmas Day or keeping the tree up until Epiphany. But when it comes to secular etiquette, there are no hard-and-fast rules about when to start (or stop) decking the halls.

“Most people find it tasteful to stick within the weeks or the month of the holiday itself,” points out Lizzie Post, co-president of The Emily Post Institute and author of Emily Post’s Etiquette—The Centennial Edition. “That said, there is no etiquette that dictates it. It’s the same way you can paint your house neon orange with blue stripes—there’s nothing that says you can’t. It’s really, do you want to be the person who does?”

For designer Tan France, the answer to that question is decidedly yes. For the past decade, he and his husband have been decorating their tree on Halloween and taking it down on Valentine’s Day. “I’m British, and we don’t really do Halloween,” he explains. “When I moved here 16 years ago, I was more obsessed with Christmas. So on Halloween day, when everyone else is crafting their costumes, I want to be decorating the tree. And a tree brings my husband and me a lot of joy when we come down and turn the lights on first thing in the morning. It’s just a lovely thing to perk your day up.”

Do people question France about his decision to put up decorations so early, and for so long? “Oh gosh, yeah, everyone asks that,” he says. “Online, the press, neighbors. And my answer is really simple: Why delay joy?”

If you are jonesing to do some holiday decorating, there's good news: While Christmas might be weeks away, there is one happening right now. It’s called Thanksgiving.

“Anytime before midnight of Thanksgiving is too early to decorate,” insists Wendy Kerrigan, founder of Atelier Design in New Orleans. “Filling the house with Christmas decorations too soon really takes away from the beauty of Thanksgiving, moving your mind into the bustle of Christmas.”

Kerrigan wishes more people would celebrate the fall season for longer; rushing to the tinsel takes away from the beauty of the autumn season, “Thanksgiving can be experienced in a special way too, filling the house with fall colors, fall leaves, gourds, and cornucopia,” says Kerrigan. “Instead of trying to hurry the year along, enjoy each family celebration for what it is.”

Even more, sprinting to the Yuletide season robs the holiday of its own magic. “What makes things special when they’re once a year is that they’re experiences you look forward to,” says Vermont-based interior designer Joshua Smith. “How many holidays can we take at one time? Are we doing Black Friday in July now? Let’s just enjoy the season that we’re in!”

When it comes to holiday decorating, many hands make light work. Several designers say they take advantage of the long holiday weekend and enforced family time to rope in some extra help making spirits bright. “In my parents' home, we start decorating right after Thanksgiving dinner. It’s a rare opportunity for everyone to be together, and my siblings and I stay to help out our parents,” says New York-based designer Mikel Welch. “Plus, it’s a much better tradition than terrorizing sales associates on Black Friday Eve!"

Smith has a plan for bringing the Christmas spirit to his home, without shouldering the burden of doing all the hall-decking himself. Last year, his family decorated their trees over Thanksgiving weekend and made it a tree-decorating contest. With his husband's family in town, “they’ll be divided into three teams, decorating three trees, and I’ll go around at the end judging, and everyone will win prizes. We’re not all going to be together at Christmas, so this activity is a fun way to celebrate the season and not do all the work myself,” he says.

Still, early decorators would argue that the amount of effort needed to set up holiday decor is another reason to put up decorations early—and keep them up late. “This is the most miserable time of the year; it gets dark so early, it’s so darn cold. I just think you should do everything you can to bring yourself joy,” says France. “Why put in all that effort for a few weeks?”

Even those who are normally on the side of waiting to decorate agree that desperate times call for dopamine decorating. Whether it’s the state of your inbox or the state of the world that is stressing you out, if you’re feeling a little blue, go right ahead and pull out the red and green.

“In a perfect calendar, the day after Thanksgiving would be my official start to the holiday season—it's traditionally the day when my family goes out into the woods of Tahoe National Forest to chop down our tree, drag it home, and decorate it,” says designer Noz Nozawa of Noz Design. “But this year, the day after Halloween is when I let the holidays start—there's plenty to worry about, and having my nutcrackers and string lights out a few weeks early can only boost spirits.”

Whenever you decorate, it can be a way to bring fun to a not-always joyful world. “We have no control over a lot of things in our lives, but when we walk through the door of our home, we choose to focus our energy and attention on whether that is on fear or on love and joy,” says Smith. “And this holiday season, I think more than ever we have to focus on ways to bring more light to ourselves and to each other.”

When it comes to early decor, “I can handle it from my neighbor; I cannot handle it from a corporation,” says Post. That may be because we know the Christmas displays popping up in a big-box store alongside the Halloween candy are trying to sell us something, while a neighbor decorating is spreading joy, spending their own time and money to brighten the dark winter nights. For a shop, holiday decorating is mercenary. For an individual, it’s a mitzvah.

If you appreciate the joy inspired by holiday displays and do want to start setting them up early or keep them up late, consider finding a middle ground by leaning toward decor that’s more about lighting up the night, rather than marking a particular occasion. “I see a lot of people in Vermont leaving the actual lights up, not so much like blow-up Santas or things like that, but the actual lights up for the entire winter,” says Post.

If you’re a minimalist (or live in a studio apartment), you don’t need a tree to decorate. “If you don’t have a tree, I’m going to invite you to place a hurricane lamp on your coffee table with a wreath of greenery around it and light a candle in the evening,” says Smith. “Because what we know from neuroscience and neuroaesthetics is, you’ve got to engage the senses to nourish the soul!” Light a candle or turn on some twinkle lights, and you’re liable to spark joy as well.

France would argue that even his trees aren’t occasion-specific. “Here’s the thing: I’m Muslim, and my tree and the lights on our house aren’t necessarily for Christmas,” he explains. “It’s to celebrate Thanksgiving, Christmas, and the New Year. This is the most miserable time of the year. It gets dark so early, it gets light so late, it’s so darn cold. I just think do everything you can to bring yourself joy.”

Though you’d never find them rushing to hang their stockings, the designer duo behind the Kansas City-based firm Kobel + Co, Elizabeth Bennett and Mallory Robins, say there’s one design scheme you can always tap into if you find yourself in the class of outliers running to get decor up before Christmas Eve. “Ralph Lauren Holiday isn't just having a moment; it's a classic holiday go-to for so many reasons,” Bennett explains. “Warm crimsons, rich browns, tactile fabrics, and blue-green plaids make a beautiful holiday palette. Layer in antique silver pieces and simple glass ornaments. A little patina and variation keep everything from feeling too matched. The goal is a warm, welcoming home with pieces that feel collected and personal.” After all, you want it to feel like home, even at Christmas.

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.

#6768. Even a Gay Assassin Can’t Escape the Damsel-in-Distress Trope on Black Doves

It’s not *better* but at least it’s different?

In many ways, Black Doves is a platonic love story between a spy, Helen (Keira Knightley), and an assassin, Sam (Ben Whishaw). But the Netflix series has plenty of room for sex and romance. You’d expect that, right? It wouldn’t be a good spy story without some kind of spice. But while Black Doves plays along with a lot of familiar spy romance tropes, it does one thing differently: it does them with men. Spoilers for Black Doves season 2 ahead.

When you watch a lot of movies and television shows within the same genre, whether for work, continuing education, or just pure love of the game, you tend to pick up on some common tropes. Sometimes, like becoming aware of a friend’s bad habits for the first time, it’s annoyingly difficult to un-notice them.

One of my least favorite tropes in the action genre, or set of tropes really, has to do with how the spy and/or assassin’s love interest invariably gets treated. These women, because they’re practically always women, are kept in the dark and therefore lack agency. The romances are inherently toxic because they lack trust! Then, the second the bad guys learn about them, these women get kidnapped or killed or otherwise put in danger to motivate and/or devastate the male hero. Boo, I say! Boring! Sexist, even! These women are not characters. They are plot devices.

In Black Doves season 1, we learn that Sam was once in a relationship with a man named Michael (Omari Douglas) that he maintained by lying about what he did for a living. When Sam’s real job follows him home, so to speak, Michael is put in a traumatic and distressing situation. Someone is shooting at him, his boyfriend is a stone-cold killer... and his boyfriend lied to him. When Michael returns, briefly, in season 2, it’s the last thing that seems to have sucked the most.

Then, in season 2, Sam tries something safer. He engages in a sexual situationship with a hotel concierge named Jerry (Samuel Barnett). On paper, it’s perfect for someone like him. Jerry is into him, but not too much. There’s no chance of them being seen in public together, because he works at the same place they hook up. It’s genuinely sweet to see Jerry try to get Sam to open up. But the second that Sam does, and invites him on a proper date, the bad guys swoop in and use Jerry as leverage. Not only does the poor concierge die for the plot, but Sam is forced to fridge his own love interest.

Is this now homophobic, instead of sexist? I would tentatively say no, for a few reasons. I covered the “Bury Your Gays” moment circa 2016, and I don’t think this qualifies there either. You can’t really say that the show thinks gay men don’t have agency, and are only fit to be plot devices when Sam, a gay man, is co-lead. And if Sam was the only queer character in the series, his inability to find happiness might be a problem; but two other assassins, Williams (Ella Lily Hyland) and Eleanor (Gabrielle Creevy), are both women and gleefully infatuated with each other. The “murder lesbians,” as another character on the show decided to label them, are in the least toxic romantic relationship on Black Doves. So, that’s something!

For what it’s worth, Helen’s husband in the series, Wallace (Andrew Buchan), is also kept in the dark for his own protection and then put in danger to motivate Helen at the end of season 2. Plus, her extramarital love interest in season 1, Jason (Andrew Koji), was quite literally killed for the plot. This really is a dangerous show for men. It’s about damn time? Forget I said that.

It is darkly refreshing (now that we’re in Woke 3, I’ve decided we can call things refreshing again) to see these tropes done on Black Doves in a way that balances the scales. I found the dozens of articles explaining why women feel safe watching “M/M romance” like Heated Rivalry to be condescending at times, and therefore I’m hesitant to admit that it’s nice for me as a woman to watch a story with these tropes that so many men seem to enjoy. I still don’t see the appeal. Nobody should be a plot device, regardless of gender. But I’m slightly less bored, and against all odds, rooting for Sam to find some peace and a lasting romance of his own.

#6767. 13 Clever Details I Saw at the 2026 Kips Bay Decorator Show House in Dallas

Check out the first ever home gym in Kips Bay Show House history.

The seventh annual Kips Bay Decorator Show House in Dallas returns to 2999 Turtle Creek Boulevard, but don’t expect it to look anything like it did in 2025. A new group of the country’s leading designers has taken on the 25,000-square-foot French Renaissance estate, transforming each room to showcase bold ideas, highlight exceptional craftsmanship, and reveal the upcoming trends we can expect to see in the world of interior design.

Visit the show house and see each of the 22 designers’ rooms, from the first dedicated home gym in Kips Bay history by Dallas-based Platinum Gym Design, to a small but mighty European-inspired kitchen by Stephenson Home, to a 1970s-inspired primary bathroom fit with a glass brick shower and curtained-off water closet by Nuance Interiors. With sponsors like Benjamin Moore, The Shade Store, The Rug Company, and more, the vibrant and personality-filled spaces come as no surprise.

As always, proceeds from the show house go to the Kips Bay Boys & Girls Club, which provides educational, enrichment, and developmental programs to thousands of young people, as well as Dallas-based nonprofits Dwell with Dignity and The Crystal Charity Ball.

To see this celebration of exceptional design, you can purchase tickets here and visit the house from Saturday, November 14th to Sunday, December 6th. However, if you can’t make it to the Lone Star State in time, check out some of our favorite moments from the event below.

Kelsi Lamberth of Nuance Interiors pulled inspiration from the 1970s for her primary bathroom, separating the shower from the main prep space using a curved glass brick wall. The sculptural aspects can be found throughout in this groovy space.

In the primary bedroom, Troy Rivington of Rivington Marx Interiors cocooned the room in a dark floral wallpaper and accessorized with other moody botanicals throughout. He was inspired by the romance of 1940s Cairo, dressing the room up like it belonged to a well-traveled collector.

As the first ever dedicated home gym at the Kips Bay Show House, Peter Hoffman of Platinum Gym Design chose a refined, luxurious setup. He named the room Tanren after the Japanese art of hammering and refining metal, and used walnut in both the equipment and the ceiling slats, stainless steel frames, and brown leather accents to complete the exercise space.

In this bespoke bathroom, Russ Peters and Bill Cates of Peters Cates Design lined the wall behind the Studio McGee x Kohler bathtub with a rich merlot tile by RIAD Tile. It’s a beautiful statement and a fabulous way to bring more texture and dimension to the room.

In this tucked away library space, filled with collected finds, designers Christina Garcia Lysaught and Grayson Knight of Layered Dimensions finished off this dark space with a stunning statement mirror. Around the perimeter are resin busts of wildlife, like owls and deer, bringing in a bejeweled touch to the room.

The small but mighty kitchen, designed by Katie Stephenson of Stephenson Home, is crowned with a crackled-gold blooming wallpaper, bringing a warm balance to the rest of the room. It’s covered in birds, butterflies, and blossoms, and it wraps the cook space in a golden glow, reminding all who enter that this room should be as beautiful as it is functional.

The citron paint color wrapping the walls in Jen Bienvenu’s entrance hall is undoubtedly enchanting and serves as the perfect backdrop for her “After the Hunt” theme, but it’s the lampshades made from old silk saris that tie everything together for us. They provide such a magical warmth and really capture the essence of a collector’s treasure room.

Related Story: Almost Every Piece in This Layered Arkansas Home Was a Lucky Antique Find

Step through the citron entry and enter the round room, designed in collaboration by Helen Bergin Interiors and Lichten Architects. The walls are softened by long curtains from The Shade Store, which create beautiful juxtaposition with the curved, sculptural silver media console by Asher Gillman. The actual turntable is flush to the surface, giving it a seamless look while it emits wonderful sound.

In the Withdrawing Room by Jen Stevens of Fonde Interiors, plaster botanicals by artist Amanda Norman come to life as they crawl from the mantel along the chimney breast, escaping to the ceiling. The vines were handmade by Norman and capture the essence of a garden left undisturbed, showing that this formal-at-first-glance room has a wilder side.

The rosy palette in designer Jen Dean’s—founder of Jede Interiors—grand bathroom is topped off with a hand-painted floral mural that covers the ceiling. The rose-gold shine reflects the natural light in the room and give it more depth, while the natural motifs spread across the rest of the space.

Inside this observatory bedroom by sisters Amy Mobley and Katie Norrid of City Hill Design, the Verellen bed is raised up from the ground on a custom platform, emphasizing the importance of the furniture’s role in this space meant for relaxation and rest. It’s wrapped in a striped silk carpet that beckons your touch with a chartreuse mohair sofa at the foot.

Related Story: A Trip to the Lone Star State Inspired the Earthy Textures of This Tennessee New Build

Colorful and collected, this vibrant library by designer Betsy Wentz is not visually quiet. The maximalist space is covered in pattern-mixed Schumacher from ceiling to floor, and the designer pumped up the volume by opting for this triangular table in this tucked away corner.

In designer Amy Switzer’s textural bar lounge—filled with plush chairs, unique artwork, large tassel accessories, and beaded cigarettes—it’s the stone details that really make the room sing. Onyx cornices crown the windows, blue agate inlays decorate the floor, and a banquette which curves around a custom stone and wood table bring a natural coolness to the room that only stone can bring.

#6766. Understated, Earthy, and Endlessly Wearable: The Best Olive Green Polishes

The earthy shade adds an edge to fall manicures while still wearing like a neutral.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Like all nail polish colors, green has a vast spectrum, one that runs from electric neon to bold kelly to soft sage and all the way down to deep, moody olive. Of all the greens out there, olive sits in a quieter place. The hue is earthy and a little mysterious, somewhere between a vintage army jacket and an actual juicy olive. It’s a shade we love for fall, since it pairs so seamlessly with black, rich browns, burgundy, red, and, of course, cream.

Once reserved for the brave few, olive has now gone mainstream. Part of the appeal is how wearable it is, offering an unexpected twist without ever feeling too loud. It’s also soft and soothing to the eye, making it an easy shade to wear day after day.

If you’re new to the shade, the easiest way in is to treat it like a neutral. Try it with a camel coat and chunky knits when the weather turns, or a crisp white shirt for something fresh and unexpected. A creamy, opaque finish feels polished and modern, while a glossy jelly or a hint of shimmer adds depth in low light. However you wear it, olive looks effortlessly considered and adds an edge to your style.

Below, we’ve rounded up the best olive green nail polishes to shop now.

A personal favorite, Elia is a muted olive green that looks rich from the very first stroke. The opaque formula glides on evenly in a single swipe and dries to a soft gloss that lasts through daily wear.

Manucurist’s take on olive green feels a touch brighter. It sits beautifully on light and dark skin tones alike and delivers an opaque wash of color. Part of the “Green Flash” collection, it cures under an LED gel lamp and lasts for nearly 21 days.

Essie’s Totally Plaid is an olive green polish with a cream finish that delivers a gel-like shine and depth while still removing as easily as a regular polish. The formula applies smoothly in two coats, giving you rich, opaque color without the need for a UV lamp.

This deep and moody olive green shade is the epitome of effortless chic, pairing easily with everything from neutral knits to bold jewel tones.

Inspired by the lush landscapes of the Mediterranean, this olive green evokes a lighter take on the olive color spectrum. It’s a tad brighter and slightly more yellow-toned, making it a great pick if you want a fresh, sunlit green this fall.

O.P.I’s Suzi is the chic olive green and wardrobe staple of the olive family. It’s a bit darker-toned, radiating earthy warmth and understated elegance. Plus, it’s fast-drying and offers up to seven days of wear.

For those seeking a little more sparkle, this olive green jelly is packed with large green holographic glitters that create a textured finish and catch the light with every movement, adding a spark of joy to your manicure.

Calming and serene, this moss green shade is part of the “Campground Collection”, a four-shade assortment of earthy, comforting colors. Its buttery-smooth application matches the shade’s peaceful look.

This may not look like your average olive green at first glance, but that’s because it has taupe undertones. Once it’s on your nails, you’ll notice the hint of green was there all along. The result is a soft, grounded neutral that pairs effortlessly with cozy fall knits and earthy accessories.

This nail color may be named Pickle, but we see the olive in there. This tangy green with muted yellow undertones feels lively while remaining neutral enough to pair with everything in your wardrobe.

A true olive green, Extra Dirty brings rich, earthy depth to your nails. All you need is one to two coats for a creamy manicure worthy of a fall-season spotlight.

#6765. Catherine Zeta-Jones’s Daughter Carys Borrows Her Mom’s Lacy Gucci Top From the ’90s

A gem from the beloved movie star’s closet

When your mom is Catherine Zeta-Jones, there is hardly a ’90s or Y2K gem you can’t get your hands on. From turn-of-the-century Fendi baguettes to red-carpet gowns (that crimson Versace dress from the 2004 Oscars particularly comes to mind), the Academy Award-winning actor embraced all the best of the decades’ trends. And now, her daughter gets to as well.

Yesterday, Carys Douglas, the 23-year-old first-born child of Zeta-Jones and husband Michael Douglas, went through her mother’s archives for a night-out look perfectly suitable for a Gucci x Moda Operandi cocktail event in New York City. Her choice was a sheer long-sleeve lace top from the Italian fashion house, with an elongated, deep U neckline secured solely by a single clasp. The piece, with its raw hems, was a familiar pull—last seen on the Chicago star at the 1996 premiere of The Long Kiss Goodnight in Los Angeles.

At the ’90s event, Zeta-Jones layered the top over a little black cami and paired it with slim trousers in the same sheer lace. She added a large cross necklace, chunky rings, and a pair of open-toe heels, and slung a large bag over her shoulder.

Three decades later, her daughter put her own spin on the piece, wearing it with a little black miniskirt and semi-sheer black tights. Douglas took a cue from her movie-star mom’s glam, wearing her hair down in slept-in waves and focusing her makeup on the lips, adding a pop of color. She grabbed a quilted Gucci bag to finish things off.

This was not the first time Douglas borrowed something from her mom’s closet. In 2024, she celebrated her 21st birthday in a pink Emanuel Ungaro slip previously seen on Zeta-Jones at the 1999 MTV Movie Awards. After posting the look on Instagram, her famous mom lovingly commented: “You deserve it sweetheart.”

#6764. Looking for a New Winter Perfume? These 4 Zara Vanilla Scents Smell So Expensive

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

There’s a reason vanilla perfumes are so popular. They’re no longer the sickly-sweet, sugar-loaded compositions you might remember. Today they’re much more complex—the creaminess of the note is balanced with more sophisticated whiffs of florals, woods, and amber. This new generation of vanilla-forward fragrances feels rounded, full-bodied, and, perhaps most importantly, expensive.

Sure, many of these complex scents come with a hefty price tag. But luckily, Zara has an entire collection dedicated to these deep vanilla perfumes, and there is an array of options that are all ideal for a comforting winter scent. With every spritz, you’ll smell how distinct each one is. In some, vanilla sits quietly at the base, gently rounding off sweeter or floral notes. In others, it takes center stage, creating a creamy, decadent throw.

Whether you like your vanilla subtle and soft for an everyday spritz, or bold and woody for after-hours, there’s a budget-friendly Zara perfume calling your name.

Notes: Chantilly cream, jasmine, sweet tonkaMood: A gourmand scent that’s indulgent and warm but never sickly

The truest vanilla of them all, Dark Romance is the place to start if you’re after a traditionally sweet, creamy—but not sickly—vanilla fragrance. The base note is sweet tonka, which creates that toasty, caramel-y base that sits on your skin, while notes of jasmine and Chantilly cream linger with a velvety, milky sillage. It’s the perfect everyday fragrance; one we envision misting on a cashmere sweater on a chilly night.

Notes: Apricot blossom, jasmine sambac, vanillaMood: A floral vanilla that’s creamy and comforting

Zara’s Hypnotic Vanilla smells exactly as it sounds. Decadent from the first spritz, it instantly envelops you in this golden, buttery cloud of vanilla. Initially, it’s sweet, but then the apricot blossom and jasmine sambac come through, giving it a floral and slightly powdery edge. If Dark Romance is the one you’d wear for a cozy night in, Hypnotic Vanilla is its older sister you’ll reach for for a night out. It’s a great option if you can never choose between gourmands and florals.

Notes: Salty coconut, jasmine, vanilla bloom, amberMood: A full-bodied gourmand that’s creamy, sweet, a little spicy, and seductive

With Immortal Vanilla, you’ll be surprised it only costs $39. While Zara’s perfumes have impressive sillage, this one truly does linger, so you’re bound to receive many compliments. On paper, it has notes of coconut, jasmine, amber, and vanilla. But IRL, they come together to create something that’s comforting, sensual, and ever so slightly creamy and salty. After one spritz, you’ll want to douse yourself in it again, and again, and again.

Notes: Wild orchid, black amber, vanilla Mood: A deep, sophisticated scent that balances vanilla with a woody base

If you want your vanilla perfume to smell like an old-fashioned, New York-style jazz bar, Daring Vanilla is for you. Of the four scents, this is the one that comes closest to a traditional oud fragrance, with naturally smoky, woody notes balanced by wild orchid and vanilla, which lend a warm, subtly sweet throw. It’s the kind of fragrance you’d slip over an LBD, slide into some killer high heels and head out for the night.

#6763. We Tested Luxury Sheet Sets for Three Years—These Are the Only Ones Worth the Splurge

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

For hot sleepers, restless sleepers, and anyone tired of scratchy bedding.

You deserve better than itchy, scratchy sheets on your bed at night. Upgrading your bedding is more than a bedroom aesthetic choice; it’s an investment in your well-being. I know this firsthand—as both a design editor and an insomniac. I’ve tested over a dozen of the best luxury sheets on the market in my journey to fix my sleep routine. The right sheet set lets me drift off much more easily, and I’m here to help you find one of your own.

What sets “luxury” sheets apart from your run-of-the-mill bargain bedding? It's a combination of texture, quality, aesthetic, and price point. The set should feel good the moment it hits your skin. The fabric, too, should be durable and constructed to avoid pilling, tearing, or any of that less-than-best feel. As the name implies, luxury sheets often come with a higher price tag, so you'll want to make sure they're worth the extra money.

I have every sleep issue you could think of: I'm a hot sleeper, I snore, I toss and turn, and I have eczema, so I have to be picky about what fabric goes in my bed. Luckily, my fellow editors, testers, and I have spent over three years sleeping on every kind of sheet under the sun, from fluffy flannel to silky eucalyptus. And now we’re revealing the ones that totally upgraded our sleep routines. Scroll on to meet our MVBs (most valuable bedding).

I first tested the Buffy Breeze sheets in my evaluation, and beyond the beautiful sheen that made the sheets look luxe, the cooling feeling of the eucalyptus fibers completely won me over. After months of trying other sheets, these were still my favorite.

I'm an incredibly hot sleeper, and I can't stand waking up to sticky sheets. These sheets from Buffy, made from moisture-wicking eucalyptus, gave me the most refreshing sleep I've had in my life. The first quality that impressed me about these sheets was how well they shifted with my constant tossing and turning. Even as they gathered underneath me, I was never uncomfortable. They're lightweight and breathable without the gauzy texture of other airy sheets. I genuinely looked forward to sinking into them at the end of the day.

I also tested Buffy's Cloud pillows under the pillowcases in this set and was impressed with how well they held their shape, even as I bunched them up under my neck. I was convinced I’d have horrible neck pain any time I slept on a pillow that wasn’t the butterfly shape I have for side sleepers. Imagine how shocked I was by how comfortable and long I slept.

Colorways: 25 | Sizes: Full, Queen, King, Cal King | Materials: Eucalyptus (lyocell)

Quince bedding is one of the best ways to get that luxury sheet feel on a tighter budget. Tester Jessica Cherner has tested their bamboo, linen, and percale varieties, but the bamboo sheets stood out for their silky feel. "These sheets are so great because they have a tiny bit of stretch to them, which makes them even softer," Cherner says. "What makes them so soft is the fact that they're made from 100 percent viscose bamboo (the pulp), which is a bit more luxurious than rayon."

Colorways: 15 | Sizes: Twin, Twin XL, Full, Queen, King, Cal King | Materials: 100 percent viscose from organic bamboo

Many die-hard fans of cotton bedding can’t imagine sleeping on anything else. The cool, crisp feeling of Hommey’s striped cotton bedding is what won over McKenzie. “These are my perfect all-purpose sheets,” McKenzie says. “It’s not for your hottest days or your coldest nights, but for all of the days that fall in between. It’s a sturdy, well-woven 100 percent organic cotton, designed for year-round use and reliable comfort. It has a crisp feel that maintains its shape, and it rarely wrinkles, even after I’ve spent all night tossing and turning. I’m also a hot sleeper, and any thread count above 400 TC starts to get a bit stuffy for my taste. These fall at a perfect 300 TC, thick enough to insulate from cool air.”

Colorways: 28 | Sizes: Twin/Twin XL, Full/Queen, King/Cal King | Materials: 100 percent organic cotton

Despite being a hot sleeper, McKenzie still needed a solid set of winter sheets for the coldest nights. The whipstitching on Boll & Branch’s flannel set made for a festive refresh in the holiday season. “I've also tested Boll & Branch's cashmere sheets, and those were more lightweight than the flannel, but they have the same brushed, soft weave,” McKenzie says. “It's as durable as my Saatva flannel sheets, but not as stiff. They look super chic, like hotel sheets. It takes a lot for me to willingly own light-colored sheets, but these are just gorgeous. My favorite detail is the holly-red whipstitching; I just wish it went all the way around the border of the set. I was sad to take these off my bed to test the others.”

Colorways: 15 | Sizes: Twin, Twin XL, Full, Queen, King w/ King Cases, King w/ Std. Cases, California King, Split King | Materials: 100 percent organic cotton

Piglet in Bed allows customers to choose between 100 percent linen and linen-blend sheets, in order to maximize personal comfort. Tester Maggie Haddad opted for the linen blend and was pleasantly surprised. “After sleeping on this bedding for more than two months, I can confidently say I don’t see myself going back to traditional cotton sheets anytime soon,” Haddad says. “I initially worried that linen might feel too coarse or scratchy for my liking, but the linen blend turned out to be the ideal balance of softness and subtle texture.”

Colorways: 13 | Sizes: Twin, Full, Queen, King | Materials: 100 percent linen or 55 percent European linen and 45 percent long staple cotton

Don’t be intimidated by cashmere’s delicate nature. Though the cleaning instructions are specific, these sheets are worth extra TLC. “Just saying ‘cashmere sheets’ has an air of luxury, and Boll and Branch's set lives up to the hype. From night one, these sheets draped over me like silk, but were more buttery soft and fuzzy than cold and smooth. As someone who sweats easily, the lightweight feel was welcome. I'd still reach for these sheets in the summer, or whenever I had a guest I wanted to impress.”

Colorways: 4 | Sizes: Queen, King with King Cases, Cal King | Materials: 92 percent extra-long-staple organic Turkish cotton, 8 percent cashmere

When contributing writer Kelsey Mulvey put these Luxome sheets to the test, every little detail wowed her, even as a sheets snob. "Unless I'm able to set the thermostat to an arctic temperature, I run hot when I sleep, so I'm always looking for bed sheets with cooling properties," Mulvey says. Not only is the viscose from bamboo used here moisture-wicking and temperature-regulating, so I'm not waking up in a pool of sweat, but they're also ridiculously silky smooth."

Colorways: 12 | Sizes: Twin, Twin XL, Full, Queen, King, Cal King, Split King | Materials: 100 percent Bamboo viscose

Colorways: 8 | Sizes: Twin, Twin XL, Full, Queen, King, Cal King, Split King | Materials: 100 percent Premium Supima cotton

They're soft and stay put, so making the bed in the morning is a breeze. And I could use them no matter the season—they strike that perfect balance of warming and cooling. It's flowy and always looks neat. Even though it's meant to optimize the comfort of a Purple mattress, I'm confident it would make any mattress brand feel softer.

It's made of 700 threads of long-staple Giza cotton, for durability and warmth. Giza cotton is a sought-after, typically expensive fabric, so it's quite the steal to get a set for under $200. After Cherner's test, she found that she loved how gentle the fabric was on her hair and skin. “Temperature-regulating and buttery soft, these sheets work for both my husband (a hot sleeper) and me (a cold one)," Dunn says. "I’ve tested a lot of sheets and what sets these apart—in addition to the cloud-like feel—is their oversized fit. The flat sheet drapes beautifully over our queen-sized bed with plenty of room for two people to be cozy, without ever fighting for coverage.”

Colorways: 17 | Sizes: Twin, Twin XL, Full, Queen, King, Cal King, Split King | Materials: Bamboo viscose

#6761. Rihanna Pairs Her Golden Jacquard Coat With a Pair of Baggy Blue Jeans

Another day, another Rihanna sighting at Giorgio Baldi. While most stars reserve their grandest looks for red carpets, galas, or film festivals, Rihanna’s preferred runway is the entrance to her favorite upscale Italian restaurant in Santa Monica, California. Over the years, the Fenty Beauty founder has used the venue to unveil some of her most-memorable looks—the sculptural white Alaïa set, the patchwork denim-and-leopard-print trench, the satin oyster-hued car coat with stacks on stacks of pearls. And she has yet to run out of ideas.

On Tuesday, Rihanna attempted a more under-the-radar visit, entering the restaurant through a tucked-away back entrance. And while her arrival may have been quiet, her outfit certainly wasn’t.

As one of the masters of high-low dressing, the “Umbrella” singer flexed her talents yet again, pairing an ornate, baroque duffle coat with a casual pair of baggy blue jeans.

Her oversize outerwear, courtesy of the Dries Van Noten Fall/Winter 2026 collection by Julian Klausner, was made with a golden floral jacquard that looked like a hanging tapestry transformed into wearable art. The piece featured a wide hood and toggle buttons in front that added a nautical spin, while around back, it featured a single vent and a martingale belt.

As a steadfast champion of a statement piece, RiRi chose a garment that was bold and fabulous, but could also help her attempt to remain incognito (a reality that’s nearly impossible when, well, you’re Rihanna).

The piece’s large hood and enveloping silhouette concealed the superstar, while in contrast, its gilded hue made her hard to miss. The coat made such a statement, in fact, that the mother of three knew she didn’t need to overwhelm it, which is why she paired it with a simple pair of barrel jeans and a gold collar necklace that complemented its metallic color. She finished things off with a pair of thin, rectangular sunglasses for a last low-key touch.

With this endorsement from Rihanna, and recent stamps of approval from other stars like Jennifer Lawrence, it appears Dries Van Noten coats are going to rule the outerwear game this season.

#6760. ‘DTWS’ Fans Are Divided Over Connor Wood and Rylee Arnold’s “Recycled” Dance-Off Choreography

Was it a clever callback or just too familiar?

I never thought I’d say this, but this week’s episode of Dancing With the Stars was a tough watch. Hear me out—it was only because my faves, Maura Higgins and Connor Wood, competed in a bonus dance-off to Sheryl Crow’s “All I Wanna Do.” I just couldn’t choose between these charming celebs. Sue me!

That being said, it was a tight race between the stars and their pro partners, Rylee Arnold and Mark Ballas. Team Tink won in the end thanks to two extra points from the judging panel, but DWTS fans seem divided over who really deserved the win after the scores were given.

“ANYONE who thought connor had a chance i hope you're feeling real embarrassed MAURA ATE HIM UP SOOOO HARD,” one person wrote, while another said, “I don’t even care that they lost (whatever) but I like watching Rylee and Connor WAYYY more.”

After the performance, Carrie Ann Inaba criticized Rylee and Connor for incorporating more country-inspired moves into their cha-cha.

“So much fun, both of you! It’s like the battle of fringe dance,” she said, before telling Connor and Rylee, “You had a lot of country line dancing. You threw that back in, which was fun for the audience, but I don’t know. It’s not a cha-cha.”

Naturally, fans took issue with Carrie Ann’s comments. “What’s the point of picking a country song if you don’t want them to incorporate that into their dance. The feedback made no sense,” another viewer said.

If that wasn’t enough to keep the debate alive, others pointed out how similar Rylee and Connor’s dance was to their week 1 cha-cha to another country song, “I Like It, I Love It” by Tim McGraw.

However, longtime fans know that repurposing choreography is the name of the game when it comes to DWTS.

One commenter on Reddit explained, “Truth be told, all the pros recycle dances for the dance-offs, which is why the concept has never really worked for me or been particularly entertaining. However, those Hee Haw moves definitely threw the entire dance-off off, and I’m glad CAI called it out. You could tell she absolutely hated every second of it.”

Either way, it doesn’t seem like Connor or Maura were phased by the online chatter after their dance-off. They actually dropped a series of comments supporting each other’s performances.

“So much fun with you guys @fibula @ryleearnold1,” Maura wrote, to which Connor responded, “we had too much damn fun I love you all.”

The podcaster even jokingly chimed in when a fan claimed Maura “cleared” him, saying, “agree that’s my queen.”

And that’s why these two are my faves—can’t wait to see them in the semifinals next week!

#6759. Zimmermann Hosts a Star-Studded Soirée in Downtown New York City

Katie Holmes, Uma Thurman, and Naomi Watts came out to celebrate the brand’s cruise 2027 collection.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Inside Indochine’s red-lit, retro-inspired French-Vietnamese dining room in New York City, Zimmermann gathered friends of the brand to celebrate its cruise 2027 collection. Presented on the sun-drenched Côte d’Azur in June, the label’s latest served as the perfect bit of vacation and occasion-ready dressing for November in New York. Now that the lineup has officially hit stores, fashion insiders and fans of the brand alike (including Uma Thurman, Eve Hewson, Katie Holmes, and Naomi Watts) showed up dripping in ruffled gowns, sailor-inspired stripes, chunky shearlings, and ethereal lace and silks from the collection. Against the dark, 1940s-inspired tropical salon interior of Indochine, it was the ideal mix of downtown energy and New York glamour.

As champagne flowed through the bar area, glasses clinked while miniature crab cakes and spring rolls were passed. Zimmermann’s sister duo Nicky and Simone Zimmermann could be spotted catching up with friends new and old. “We have a very, very close connection with New York,” Nicky told ELLE in between greeting guests. “It was pretty much when I started the business, one of the first places internationally that I went to in the ’90s, and it blew my mind. It’s been inspirational to us 100 percent.” It was a rare occasion for the brand; for years, Zimmermann has shown at Paris Fashion Week (after presenting in New York in the mid-2010s) and the duo oscillates between Sydney and Paris for most of the year.

Zimmermann’s painterly and intrinsically bohemian flourishes could be seen in the many outfits of guests, all highlighting the vivid cruise collection. Think: decadent silky draped drop-waisted gowns and paneled lace maxi dresses, or, for the more demure, sleek all-black looks that captured the New York essence. “For me, there’s a little bit of a sense of humor behind it—without insulting anybody—it was about the story when Australia actually beat America in the America’s Cup, which is a very famous sailing race,” Nicky told ELLE of the collection’s inspiration.

“I think it was in 1983, and America had not lost the America’s Cup in something like 173 years; it was a ridiculous winning streak, and Australia came along. The inspiration is more about a David and Goliath kind of story,” she continued. “We came along as the Aussies, not established in the sailing world. They were very cheeky, rogue, kind of Aussie young guys up against this amazing, sophisticated establishment of the New York Yacht Club. And they never lost—and we beat them. So in Australia, it’s a legendary story.” Nicky combined the sportiness of sailing gear with the sort of elite New York Yacht Club preppiness, resulting in colorful silk scarf embellishment or sweaters decked out in accessorized pins. “They would hold the most incredible parties and the women were so glamorous and sophisticated and beautiful and not anything that Australia could relate to at the time, so I loved the contrast.”

Conversation was flowing and blooming anthuriums covered the tables as guests took their seats. Nicky kicked off the dinner with a speech, musing on her early days in New York on the Lower East Side and the many parties she attended in Tribeca before sharing more inspiration behind the collection: “It’s about establishment, and then it’s about people, you know, working really hard, coming together as a team, defeating the undefeatable,” she said, with a laugh.

Next came an intimate performance by the singer and Bad Monkey actress Charlotte Lawrence, who sang two of her original songs, then led a folksy sing-along to “Dreams” by Fleetwood Mac. Even the most guarded of celebs and fashion’s elite couldn’t help but bob their heads and sing a tune. As guests dined on a family-style mix of kale salad, spicy shrimp, grilled salmon, barbecued chicken, and steak, the champagne glass clinks slowed as candles slowly flickered and the evening closed out with lemon tarts.

A little yacht-club polish, a little Aussie irreverence: Zimmermann celebrated cruise 2027 in high style.

#6758. From “Magnetic Merlot” to “Soft Licorice,” 10 Dark, Minimalist Manicure Ideas to Try This Winter

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Bolder hues are getting the starring role as winter sets in

While soap manicures, subtle petal nails, and longevity techniques once dominated the summer months, a darker color palette is quickly taking over now that the clocks have gone back and evenings are early. Yet if you're hesitant to venture into "too bold" territory or fear a pop of color on your fingertips, a dark manicure doesn't have to feel overly attention-grabbing.

In fact, as professional nail technician Samantha Rudge shares, dark manicures with a more minimalist finish are proving to be winter's most popular manicure trend. "For this season, I think we'll be seeing darker classic manicures with minimalist touches," she shares, adding that she has already seen an uptick in shade requests for Bio Sculpture's rich, chocolate-toned Decadent Cocoa and the ever-popular Garnet Noir, a moody red-black hue. "Nail designs like chrome, cat eye, micro French tips, and tortoiseshell will also dominate this season," she adds.

"Short nails are also very popular right now, no matter the nail art or shades," she adds. Regardless of shape or color, Rudge stresses the importance of prioritizing nail health at this time of year. To protect nail longevity and prevent chipping, Rudge recommends assessing your natural nail condition, choosing the right base (BIAB or gel), and using a nourishing cuticle oil to repair and strengthen brittle nails.

Ahead, discover 10 new dark manicures for those who love a more minimalist finish, from “Garnet Noir” nails to subtle lines.

Somewhere between a square and an oval, these squoval nails look sophisticated and smart—plus, paired with the deep red “garnet noir” shade, they make a strong case for opting for a shorter length.

The epitome of minimalist nail art, add subtle intrigue to your manicure this winter via a curved line created on the cuticles. Choose complementary shades to recreate it: think cherry red with hot pink; navy blue and khaki green.

Taking over the SS27 runways this season, purple is finally having a moment in the spotlight. When it comes to nail colors, look for richer hues with a jewel-tone finish to keep the manicure elevated and modern. Rudge recommends Bio Sculpture's Erica shade.

All shades of wine are a staple as the festive season approaches, but why not add a chrome effect for extra sparkle?

Coffee tones are becoming one of the most popular color stories in nail salons this winter. These teeny-tiny dots make for a unique switch-up and can be created using a micro dotting tool.

A subtle marble effect elevates an otherwise classic burgundy manicure.

While almond and square are among the most popular of nail shapes, don't underestimate a soft oval. Paired with a classic cabernet tone, this dark manicure strikes the balance of looking impactful yet soft.

If you're taking a break from acrylics or longer lengths, a super-short licorice manicure is the way to do it. Add a glossy top coat and keep the nails short and square.

In the mood to experiment? Simply switch up each nail with chocolate swirls, block colors, or a French tip to recreate this look.

This ultra-glossy midnight blue manicure is all about a high-shine, mirrorlike finish that could be created with a chrome powder or top coat.

#6757. Spice Up Your Mani with Cinnamon Brown Nails This Fall

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

At this point, we all know that a brown manicure is a fall staple. Even as I type this right now, I’m wearing one that’s a week old (in DND Spiced Brown, if you’re wondering). Espresso nails? Love. Chocolate brown nails? Yep. Chai latte nails? A lighter way to do things. But, it turns out we haven’t exhausted every way to wear the shade quite yet. The latest brown look to get on my radar is cinnamon nails, the warm, spicy take on the fall-friendly color. And honestly, with the holidays around the corner, the smell of cinnamon is sure to be wafting through my kitchen for the next couple months, so the timing couldn’t be better.

These warm, spicy nails combine rich brown with subtle red and copper undertones for a cozy, cinnamon-inspired shade that feels tailor-made for autumn. Ahead, with the help of celebrity nail artist Stephanie Stone (she’s Sally Hansen’s nail style director and has worked with lots of your favorite stars—ahem, Charli XCX) I’ll break down everything you need to know about trending cinnamon brown nails, including plenty of inspo pics, tips for getting the look, and polish recommendations.

As the name suggests, cinnamon brown nails are inspired by the warm, reddish-brown hue of the spice itself. It’s warmer than a classic brown and less red than a terracotta, sitting right in between the two. While espresso and chocolate brown nails tend to lean darker and cooler, these bring some toasty and earthy vibes to your fingertips. While a classic creamy polish looks great in cinnamon, the shade also offers plenty of opportunities to play around with the finish, like with a dimensional velvet cat eye manicure or a chrome topper.

The latter is one of Stone’s favorite ways to wear the trend—she reaches for Sally Hansen’s Miracle Gel in Chics in Charge to get the effect. “Think the glazed donut’s cousin coming to visit for winter break,” she says. And especially apt for the holidays, you can add a little dazzle to the shade: “If you want to give them some extra sparkle, add a crystal at the base of your nails for an extra catch of light,” she adds.

Stone puts it perfectly: “Every great manicure starts with immaculate cuticle prep and nail care.” Before reaching for any polish, “trim and file your nails to their desired shape,” she says. “Then use Sally Hansen’s Push & Buff Cuticle Pen to clean your nail plate of an excess dead skin.” This helps clean up your cuticles easily and quickly. Next, “wash your hands with soap and water and wipe your nail beds with alcohol,” she adds, noting how important this final prep step is, especially if you’re doing a metallic finish. “Metallic shimmer and polishes expose any texture or dust you may have on your nails,” she explains.

Then, it’s polish time. This part is pretty straightforward: Paint your nails with two coats of your favorite beige or brown nail polish, she says, recommending a medium brown shade “if you want your nail look to be reminiscent of a cinnamon roll” or a reddish brown “if you want your nails more reminiscent of cinnamon as the spice itself.” After letting dry for a few minutes, add top coat and cuticle oil to finish things off. Need some product recs? Stone’s go-to polish is Sally Hansen’s Miracle Gel Polish in Been There, Dune That, but you can also get the look with Olive & June’s Nail Polish in Sassy Molasses or Essie’s Gel Couture Liquid Diamonds in Marquise Please too.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmo. She’s covered and tested all things nails, from the coolest new polish colors to cuticle care and nail art trends.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan and was previously the beauty assistant at Good Housekeeping, Woman's Day, and Prevention.

#6756. Culture Watch: What to See and Where to Go in November

Culture Watch is a series spotlighting the intersection of fashion and art, bringing you the latest must-see exhibitions, pop-ups, installations, and more.

November is a good month to make a little room in your calendar. As the holiday season starts to gather pace, galleries, brands, and creative spaces are offering plenty of reasons to get out and look around. In New York, Salon Art + Design returns for its 15th year, while Loro Piana presents its “Master of Cashmere” installation at The Brant Foundation; farther afield, international exhibitions offer even more to explore. Check out all of this month’s best cultural happenings below.

Louis Vuitton Presents ‘La Valise Bruno Aveillan’ at LV Dream

For 13 years, photographer and filmmaker Bruno Aveillan has taken his Louis Vuitton Alzer suitcase to more than 100 destinations, making it a recurring presence in his work. Now, the well-traveled case is at the center of “La Valise Bruno Aveillan,” an immersive exhibition at LV Dream, the house’s encompassing destination in Paris. Aveillan’s photographs appear alongside historic Louis Vuitton trunks and a closer look at the craftsmanship behind the Alzer, the brand’s hard-sided suitcase built around a sturdy wooden frame. The exhibition marks the final chapter in the house’s yearlong celebration of the monogram’s 130th anniversary.

Loro Piana Names Its Cashmere of the Year and Unveils a ‘Master of Cashmere’ Installation

Loro Piana put cashmere in the spotlight at The Brant Foundation in New York, where it marked the 11th edition of its Cashmere of the Year Award. Established in 2015 and held stateside for the first time, the prize recognizes the herders who produce the finest cashmere fiber. Coinciding with the honor, which went to three couples of cashmere herders from Inner Mongolia, is “Master of Cashmere: The Quest for Fiber Excellence,” an installation that traces the material from its origins to the Maison’s work with it. The multi-room experience features archival sketches, explanatory timelines, and, of course, soft-to-the-touch cashmere.

Salon Art + Design Returns to the Park Avenue Armory for Its 15th Year

The 15th edition of Salon Art + Design is now on view at the Park Avenue Armory—and it’s one of its most immersive, expansive editions yet. As is tradition, the event brings together collectible design, fine art, and jewelry. The event is open through November 8. Standouts include Jean Dunand’s 1929 La Forêt lacquered screen, Michel Dufet’s 1918 daybeds, and Jean Royère’s illuminated desk. Fernando Mastrangelo’s totem lamps add a sculptural note, while the jewelry lineup includes pieces from Fernando Jorge, Mellerio, Cora Sheibani, and Viltier.

Lara Saget Debuts Her First New York Solo Show, ‘And the World Came Apart’

Lara Saget brings more than a decade of material experimentation to her first solo exhibition in New York. At Trotter&Sholer’s new Elizabeth Street space, her sculptures and wearable works fuse glass, stone, and metal through heat, pressure, and time. Drawing on geological processes and NASA data, the show moves from ancient natural formations to humanity’s rapid impact on the planet.

The Phillips Collection Opens ‘Avery, Gottlieb & Rothko: By the Sea’

Milton Avery went to Gloucester, Massachusetts, to “live like an artist,” then brought Adolph Gottlieb and Mark Rothko along for summers of creative exchange. In Washington, D.C., The Phillips Collection’s “Avery, Gottlieb & Rothko: By the Sea” brings together watercolors and color-drenched canvases, tracing what those Cape Ann days set in motion.

Tate St Ives Spotlights Wilhelmina Barns-Graham

What does a landscape feel like in the body? Scottish artist Wilhelmina Barns-Graham spent eight decades exploring that question through art shaped by sensation and memory. The retrospective at Tate St Ives in Cornwall, England, is the most comprehensive survey of her work, moving from early landscapes and studio interiors to later works alive with bold color and sweeping gestures.

Taylor Swift, take note: famously hard-to-get-into New York City hotspot The Corner Store has officially landed at The Cosmopolitan of Las Vegas, marking its first-ever location outside Manhattan. Expect its adventurous martinis, signature wood-paneled interiors by Rockwell Group, and a handful of Vegas-exclusive menu additions, including a Butcher’s Counter serving up wagyu tomahawk and porterhouse steaks.

#6754. I Never Imagined These UGGs Could Look This Fashionable, but Tate McRae Proved Me Wrong—Shop the Boots Here

Wasn’t expecting these to become my new favorite shoes...

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Shoe styles come and go, but UGGs are forever. They’ve stood the test of time through some brutal trend cycles and somehow remained a closet staple through it all. Some designs have had their moment in the spotlight, while others... well, let's just say I wouldn't be mad if they stayed in the past. But I really thought the tall boots were stuck in my 2010s high school days (let alone the ribbon ones). And then Tate McRae posted an Instagram picture wearing them, proving me wrong real quick because why did I immediately run to add them to my cart? In the big 2026! Her power!!

In the pop star’s most recent photo dump, one of the outfits made me do a double-take. She’s wearing a cropped tee, micro athleisure shorts, angel wings (which I’m going to assume are leftovers from her Victoria’s Secret rehearsal days), and, of course, the iconic UGG boots—a footwear choice I definitely wasn’t expecting!

Personally, I still get the shivers thinking about how I used to style these. Skinny jeans and a random sweatshirt were pretty much my go-to combination. But after seeing how Tate styles them—even if her approach leans more laid-back—I'm starting to see them in a whole new light. It really comes down to proportions. I love that she gave them a sporty, '90s-inspired vibe by pairing them with micro shorts to show off their length. But now that we're heading into the chillier season, I think they'd be just as fun styled with baggy jeans or pants tucked in, leggings, a silk midi or bubble mini skirt, or even a sweater dress to lean into that cozy aesthetic.

The fur trim along the top and back of the boot, paired with the chocolate brown ribbons, adds such a whimsical touch to the classic boot. If you're into the coquette aesthetic, these are definitely for you. But Tate also proves that you can take them in a completely different direction and give them a cool, street-style edge. And perhaps the best part? They'll actually keep you warm through the colder months ahead. Catch me wearing these all the way through March!

#6753. A New Marni x Hoka Sneaker Is Here—Here’s What to Know

The Italian fashion house once again brings its unique touch to the athletic brand.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Last year, when Marni and Hoka launched their first collaboration, the two labels came together to bring the world a collection of puffy, chunky, candy-colored takes on the athletic footwear brand’s Bondi sneaker. Naturally, it was a hit among the streetwear set, including the likes of Steph Curry. Of course, a lot can change in the span of a year and a half—and the latest sneaker trends are no exception. In the same way jeans have gone from baggy styles to straight-leg and even skinny jeans, fashion has taken its sneakers from their dad eras to silhouettes that lean a little sleeker. The latest result of these turning tides: The Hoka Clifton Marni.

“Shifting from the maximalist and oversized aesthetic towards a calmer, more essential form made sense with Meryll [Rogge]’s ascension to Marni,” Thomas Cykana, Hoka’s senior director of global collaborations, tells ELLE in an email. “This affords a simple but surprising design that cues premium embellishments, metallic hardware, and unexpected prints over an overtly in-your-face design. This shift enables consumers to both craft their uniform and express their individuality without sacrificing Hoka’s pure performance capabilities.”

Available now, the design is the culmination of form and function. Marni brings its unique design perspective to the technical shoe, not only with the chosen colors—Orange, Black, and White—but also with design-forward touches such as woven heel tabs, windowpane-patterned insoles, and three shoelace options for each colorway.

“Marni has a unique ability to take everyday design cues and make them feel artful and unexpected. That lust for curiosity and imaginative nature is what first drew our team to the label and continues to inspire us,” Cykana shares. “Our debut collection in 2025 showed how much our teams share in creativity and craftsmanship, and that trust led us to our unique spin on the iconic Clifton. The editorial tone they delivered to this project reflects the perfect sensation of everyday and extraordinary at once.”
