#6752. The 10 Best Hair Wavers Bazaar Editors Are Actually Using

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Get beach hair all year round with these top-rated tools

As someone with pin-straight Asian hair, I have always admired waves and curls. I have slick, straight, stubborn hair, and—most frustratingly—it resists holding a curl. I’ve tried every trick in the book: extra-hold mousses, setting my curls with pins, and the best strong-hold hairsprays. Nothing seemed to do the trick—until I discovered hair wavers, specialized curling irons that create bends instead of twists.

In recent years, hair wavers have grown in popularity because they're easy to use and deliver effortless-looking results. “The effect [of a waver] is less curled and more textured. It’s great for that effortless, tousled vibe or adding body to finger hair without that much bounce,” says celebrity hairstylist Danielle Priano. Ahead, Priano shares her picks for the best hair wavers, along with some of the Bazaar beauty team’s favorites.

Every time my hairstylist uses this waver at the salon, I notice that my waves can stay for up to three to four days, which is considered a long time for my hair type. The waves also look so natural that they give me that “just permed my hair” effect. Priano is a fan of the tool too, saying, “It heats evenly and creates long-lasting waves with a sleek finish.” Plus, it has tons of features like a two-hour auto shutoff and a clear digital display.

You may have seen this on social media, and for good reason: At under $50, it is a great option for budget-conscious users. It’s also highly rated for both long and short hair and is easy (and safe!) for beginners to use because it has no exposed barrel. We also love that it comes in 1- and 1.25-inch wave sizes, so you can choose the wand that best fits your length and style preference.

Priano also swears by Trademark Beauty’s double-barrel wave iron. “This one’s loved for its wider barrels and even heat distribution,” she says. “It’s great for longer hair or when you want that soft glam wave.” As a bonus, the tool also works well for shorter hair as it creates two waves rather than the usual three.

This is my go-to hair waver at the moment because it’s so versatile. You can adjust the barrel size to get the exact wave you want, from tight to soft and beachy. (And you also have full control over the temperature.) I’m super lazy, so I like to opt for something that has full capabilities in one.

You can’t beat this price, which makes Bed Head’s tool a great entry-level styler. That, along with its tourmaline ceramic barrels, 10 heat settings, and easy digital controls, makes it super straightforward to use. The barrels themselves are also on the large side, which creates a great beachy effect in the final look. And if you’re accident-prone, take comfort in the fact that it comes with a protective glove, too.

“This waver is pricey, at nearly $200, but it’s also the most versatile. It comes with three barrels—a waver, a rod, and a clamp—so you can create any type of wave or curl your heart desires,” says Bazaar beauty director Jenna Rosenstein. “I have thick hair, and it works just as well on my hair as it does on those with fine, thin hair, since the heat is customizable up to 450 degrees. It’s also great to travel with!”

This one is another Bazaar editor favorite: “While I do own two different hair wavers, they both serve a unique vibe. I love this INH Triple Barrel when I’m going for a looser, more lived-in wave,” says Sarah Olivieri, Bazaar senior designer. “It really gives the illusion of salty beach hair freshly dried from the sun.”

“I love the look of effortless waves, but creating seamless waves without creases or harsh demarcations between crimps can be tricky,” says Bazaar beauty market and commerce editor Katie Intner. “I love the jumbo Mane waver because the large barrel size gives a more natural wave and crimps hair in under a few minutes. Plus, it’s dual voltage, which is great for travel, and gives a surprising amount of wave longevity.” Even better, the base is detachable and can be used with any of the brand’s other hair tool attachments.

This waver is on the pricey side, but that’s because it has the brand’s next-gen features. Think: even heating and a curved—not cylindrical—shape on the barrels that gives that ideal “effortless” look. They’re also finished with an anodized coating that adds shine and reduces frizz. Who doesn’t want that?

If you aren’t a bona fide hairstyling expert and want a hair waver that doesn’t require much technique, this option from WavyTalk might be a good fit. It has a built-in timer you can set from four to 10 seconds that beeps when it’s time to move on to the next section of hair. Its deep V-shaped plates and dual-barrel design sculpt hair for a more pronounced wave.

Luckily, it’s “fairly straightforward,” Priano says. “You start by sectioning your hair; the smaller the section, the more defined the wave. Then, working from the roots down, clamp the waver onto the hair and hold for a few seconds, usually 5 to 10, depending on your hair type and heat setting. Move down the same strand, overlapping slightly to continue the wave pattern all the way to the ends. Repeat throughout the entire head, and once cooled, you can brush it out for a softer, more lived-in texture or leave it defined for more drama.”

What’s the Best Way to Prep Your Hair Before Using a Waver?

To minimize potential damage, you’ll want to do a few things first: “Always start with clean, dry hair; wavers are not meant for wet styling. I recommend applying a heat protectant from mid-lengths to ends to shield the hair from damage,” Priano adds. “If you’re looking to boost volume and hold, add a lightweight texturizing spray or a mousse before blow-drying. And don’t forget to detangle. Smooth, tangle-free strands help the waver glide better and create more uniform results.”

Is There a Difference Between a Waver and a Curling Iron?

“The main difference between a hair waver and a curling iron lies in the shape and result. A curling iron wraps hair around a single barrel, creating more classic, rounded curls or ringlets, depending on the size of the barrel and the technique,” Priano says. “A waver, on the other hand, presses the hair between barrels to form deep, wave-like ridges.”

#6749. How to Layer Clothes for Fall and Winter Without Sacrificing Style

Fall is officially here and I am doing everything to stay warm. Layering is a very necessary skill come the colder months of the year. Not only is it practical—keeping you warm and such—but it also makes your fall and winter outfits that much more interesting.

As a Florida native now in the Northeast, layering is something I’m just now getting the hang of. And if you’re like me, either new to the cold or been here for years and can’t quite get it, I’ve got you covered. Below, I’ve gathered a few fall layering styling tips so you can lean into the fun of it all.

One of my favorite cozy pieces in my closet is an oversized turtleneck sweater. Not only will it keep you warm, but it is very chic. A fun way to elevate what could be seen as a simple look is tying your sweater around your waist, but specifically off to the side. It adds an undeniable touch of cool.

When first getting into layering, you can always start small. And doubling up on your shirts is the easiest way to achieve warmth. Raise the stakes by showing off those layers, either by wearing a longer shirt underneath or slightly raising the top shirt so the one underneath is visible. Even more points if the shirts are different colors so you get a color-blocking moment. Add in a peekaboo jacket zipped similarly to the look above and you’re set.

Belt layering is definitely more for the styling side of fall/winter layering, but it’s just as important. When trying to bring a little more life to a cold weather look, belt stacking is a fashion-forward option I personally love.

Oversized jackets are the real winners of fall. Especially a leather jacket like the one above. Add in a patterned sweater tied around the waist for a little more fun and you’ve got a perfect look.

Similar to tying around the waist, adding a scarf over top of your coat is another more style-forward layering tip that might not necessarily provide all the warmth, but brings the style.

A true Y2K favorite of mine that works great for the colder months is dresses over jeans. If you’re into nostalgia fashion, this is a more modern way to tap into the trend, while also staying warm.

She is an award-winning journalist from Miami, Florida, and a graduate of the historically Black university, Florida A&M. #6748. The 12 Best Round Brushes for a Salon-Level Blowout

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Perfect your next hair look with these expert-loved picks

Perfecting an at-home blowout once required contortionist-level abilities combined with a little patience and a lot of skill, especially when styling the back of your head. But nowadays, thanks to the advancement in hair tools—from high-powered blow-dryers to one of the best round brushes—it's easier than ever to craft sleek styles that rival your local salon without dealing with arm fatigue.

Unlike regular boar-bristle or vented brushes, round brushes feature a cylindrical shape in which hair wraps around for a seamless dry. Though many of these tools are crafted from similar materials, they're also available in a range of sizes to suit your hair texture and unique beauty needs. But regardless of the brush you choose, experts say a polished dry isn't achieved with dripping wet hair. "The key is to get all the moisture out," celebrity hairstylist Priscilla Valles tells Bazaar. "To achieve the smooth look, you need to keep drying until the hair is completely dry."

New York City-based hairstylist Jackson Simmonds recommends having two to three round brushes in your collection—just in case you change your hairstyle. “To keep it simple, think, smaller brushes for shorter lengths, bigger brushes for longer lengths,” he explains.

Here, we've narrowed down some of the best round brushes from Oribe, Sisley Paris, and more, all highly recommended by experts. Whether your hair is short, extra long, curly, or super fine, these top picks can be game-changing to help deliver a bouncy, full-bodied blowout from the comfort of your bathroom.

When we polled hairstylists, several mentioned Olivia Garden's hairbrushes. They win best overall round brush because they're foolproof: They come in a full range of sizes (general rule of thumb: the longer your hair, the larger and longer your barrel should be), and it's hard to get your hair tangled in an Olivia Garden round brush. If you need a place to start, the Ceramic + Ion Thermal Hairbrush is a bestseller. But you can also go through the brand's hairbrush guide to find your perfect fit.

Bristle type: syntheticBarrel material: ceramicSize options: 0.5 inches to 4.25 inches

A boar-bristle round brush is ideal for someone with coarse hair texture, as it provides more stretch and tension than a ceramic or ionic brush. This line is a favorite of stylists like Simmonds, who says, “They have a huge selection of sizes, bristle density, and length, so you have an option for whatever style you're trying to create.” He adds that the cork handle is a plus: “It makes it light and breezy in your hand and never slips away as you're working.”

Bristle type: nylonBarrel material: woodSize options: 35 to 85 millimeters

Oribe crafted a brush that's as efficient as it is pretty. Atop the stunning resin handle is a mix of boar bristles and nylon pins that move through hair without damaging or tugging and help distribute the natural oils in hair for more shine. And although it is an investment (the handle is hand-finished), this is a tool you’ll use for years to come.

Bristle type: boar, nylonBarrel material: Italian resinSize options: medium, large

If you're in search of a round brush with frizz-control benefits, look no further than this top-rated, ergonomic favorite from Drybar. After all, who better to go to than the blowout blueprint? Its bristles are optimized with ionic technology to mitigate frizz while providing salon-level tension control. It even has a thumbprint grip to help maximize your styling experience.

Bristle type: boarBarrel material: ceramicSize options: medium, large

If you're already a fan of Moroccanoil's coveted hair staples, allow us to introduce you to its top-rated ceramic and ion-infused round brush. It heats up when in use with a blow-dryer, making drying and detangling hair a breeze. Designed for medium to long hair, the large barrel covers a good amount of surface area without tugging. Plus, the ceramic finish leaves hair extra-shiny.

Bristle type: nylon, ionicBarrel material: ceramicSize options: 55 millimeters, 45 millimeters

This $14 round brush has earned a five-star rating from beauty lovers. The bristles are heat-resistant vegan boar bristles, and they're pretty densely packed, so you can get a grip and lift on the hair while still allowing strands to glide through without pulling. The bristles also aren’t overly stiff, so they won’t scratch your scalp as you’re styling. And we’re always here for a no-slip grip!

Bristle type: vegan boarBarrel material: bambooSize options: 2.78 inches

These U.K.-made brushes are celebrity stylist Benjamin Mohapi’s personal favorites. “They are the oldest brush company in the world, and I've used them for the entire 40 years of my career,” he explains, adding that the brand has options in boar bristle, vegan bristle, and nylon ceramic. “As far as I’m concerned, there is nothing better.”

Bristle type: nylonBarrel material: ceramicSize options: 45mm and 60mm

Here's a brush from the experts at Sisley Paris that's literally made for your next blowout. Its boar and nylon bristles are gentle yet effective, and compactly housed in a wooden barrel. The soft bristles provide hold for easy drying and also keep the hair super soft. We also appreciate that it has scalp-stimulating bristles, so it feels especially nice when you use it at the root.

Bristle type: boar, nylonBarrel material: woodSize options: medium

This popular round brush proves that getting a polished, salon-level finish doesn't have to cost a ton. Ideal for reducing frizz, the staple uses a ceramic-coated barrel for even heat distribution. Plus, it features an ergonomic handle for extra comfort, which you’ll love whether you’re new to blowing out your hair at home or just need the extra grip. Once you get the hang of things, you may decide to upgrade, but it’s a great starter brush.

Bristle type: nylonBarrel material: ceramicSize options: 1.7 inches, 1.3 inches

The three sturdy knobs on the handle of this round brush mean you don't have to worry about the tool accidentally slipping out of your hands mid-blow-dry. Plus, the placement of the bristles in Philip B.'s tool makes it easy to give your roots an extra lift. With a mix of boar bristles and nylon for extra reinforcement, it’s a sturdy brush that’s earned stellar reviews for good reason.

Bristle type: boar, nylonBarrel material: mahogany Size options: large, medium

Can a round brush be chic? This one could be mistaken for a found object with its teak handle. Simmonds recommends the Japanese-made brand, saying that it offers “quality brushes that deliver results, look great coming out of my kit, and last for ages with proper care.” The mix of boar bristle and heat-resistant nylon pins is also ideal for optimal hold and shine.

Bristle type: boar, heat-resistant nylonBarrel material: teak woodSize options: several

Balmain Hair may sound like a fun splurge (and it is), but Simmonds says the brand has become a go-to for his ceramic tools. “Their range is every bit as luxurious as you would expect,” he says. This brush in particular helps seal the cuticle for an extra-smooth and shiny blowout, and can even work for extra-long hair. Have short hair? Try the 25mm version for the same luxury experience.

Bristle type: syntheticBarrel material: ceramic coatingSize options: 25mm–53mm

Like anything worth learning, styling your hair with one of the best round brushes takes practice. "Ask your stylist if they’ll do a lesson," offers Gamez. It also might be helpful to reference a YouTube or TikTok tutorial. The general guidance is: start fresh out of the shower, detangle your hair, and then begin blow-drying and brushing small sections of hair, working your way around your head. You can start in the front or back sections. Professional hair colorist Tylor Johnson recommends starting with the front sections around your hairline, as your hairline pieces are the most important and require the most precision, and you don't want to be fatigued when styling them.

What Are the Benefits of Using a Round Brush and Blow-Dryer?

It’s all about the shape. “The round brush allows the hair to wrap around it, creating a bend in the hair as it dries and cools,” explains Mohapi, who explains that a regular hairbrush should not be used for heat-styling. “Expensive brushes like Masons are not designed for direct heat, and you will ruin a perfectly beautiful brush,” says the stylist.

Several top stylists prefer to use round brushes and blow-dryers separately rather than an all-in-one device, as it enhances control while minimizing hair damage. "Blow-dry brushes tend to be high heat, so if you’re putting that tool on soaking wet hair, you’re going to overdry and damage it," explains Gamez. "A blow-dryer allows you to create space between the heat and the hair shaft as needed. You can control the air direction of a dryer with your nozzle, pointing downward and sealing the cuticle, resulting in shinier, smoother hair, as opposed to a hot brush that blows air outward in all directions."

On the other hand, blow-dry round brushes definitely have their benefits. According to Valles, one key factor is their overall ease of use to craft salon-worthy styles at home. "A blow-dry brush makes the process so much easier," Valles says. "Some people aren't as coordinated handling a brush and dryer simultaneously, but the blow-dry brush gives the same results and is easier to manage." At the end of the day, the best round brush for you is one you actually enjoy using regularly.

“Many people wouldn’t want to use a boar bristle brush for ethical reasons, and there are lots of good alternatives, so boar bristle isn’t the only option,” explains Mohapi. “It is, however, a highly effective material and is used extensively in hairbrush manufacturing globally.” Here’s why: “Boar bristles are highly tolerant of heat and won’t melt or buckle under the intense extremes of a packed Friday at the salon. The fibers are both strong and flexible and have the unique quality of being able to almost grab the hair and hold it in place as you pass it through the brush.”

That said, the stylist adds that some synthetics are nearly as good. They just may not last as long. “Nylon is a different thing altogether,” he notes. “Nylon will close down the cuticle and help create a shiny, slick finish to the hair. This is great for straight hair, but nylon brushes take all the body out of the hair, so when you use a nylon round brush, it has a different type of finish.”

#6747. The Secret to Kendall Jenner’s ‘Me Mode’

The model and founder shares her favorite ways to unwind and how the Therabody TheraFace Mask Glo fits into her nightly routine.

For Kendall Jenner, “Me Mode” is a time to unplug from the daily demands of work and social obligations and simply be still. “I have a really busy mind, so switching off has never come naturally to me,” says the model and entrepreneur. “I’ve had to build little rituals that help me slow down.”

One of those rituals involves Therabody’s TheraFace Mask Glo, an LED face mask that bridges skincare with wellness. Delivering full LED coverage across the entire face, including the lips and under eyes, the cordless device features 504 red, infrared, and blue LEDs, plus a relaxing scalp massage to help relieve tension. Best of all, treatment time is only 12 minutes, giving Jenner a quick, easy, and much-needed pause from her busy schedule.

“After a long day, it’s sweatpants, my dogs, and zero plans,” Jenner says. “Putting on the Mask Glo has kind of become my signal that the day is over. For those 12 minutes, my brain finally goes quiet.”

One thing Jenner loves about the TheraFace Mask Glo is how effortlessly it fits into her routine. “It’s hands-free, so I just put it on and do whatever I feel like that night,” Jenner says. “Sometimes that’s music or journaling, and a lot of the time it’s doing absolutely nothing. The scalp massage is my favorite part. It feels less like a skincare step and more like actually relaxing.”

It offers legitimate skincare benefits, too. Backed by the largest clinical study of any LED mask on the market, the TheraFace Mask Glo was shown to reduce under-eye wrinkles and fine lines, enhance radiance, and lift sagging skin in as little as eight weeks.

The TheraFace Mask Glo offers three LED settings to target different skin concerns. Red light helps improve the appearance of fine lines, wrinkles, and dark spots, while red and infrared light work together to boost collagen production. Blue light targets acne-causing bacteria, helping prevent future breakouts.

The key is consistent use, which Jenner has found easy to maintain. “If a routine is too complicated, I’m not going to stick with it, and I’ve learned that consistency matters more than anything,” she says. “With the Mask Glo, I pick red, red and infrared, or blue light depending on how my skin is acting, and that’s it. It’s easy enough that I actually use it, which is half the battle for me. It comes with me when I travel now, too.”

The LED lights are placed across the entire face, even covering overlooked areas like the mouth and eyes. The preset treatment runs for 12 minutes, cycling through four minutes of each light setting. Four-minute individual treatments are also available.

Combining advanced skincare and relaxation into one simple step, the TheraFace Mask Glo is ideal for anyone who wants to level up their nighttime routine with minimal effort. As Jenner has learned over the years, less is more when it comes to beauty and wellness.

“I’ve been sitting in makeup chairs since I was 14, so I went through a phase of trying everything,” she says. “Now I do a lot less, and I’m more intentional about what I put on my skin. I try to give it a break when I can, and I’ll use the Mask Glo before glam on shoot days.”

Jenner’s nighttime routine is just one aspect in living a more holistic lifestyle. “Wellness for me is mostly about balance now: being at the barn with my horses, getting outside, and protecting my nights,” she says. “My friends make fun of me for going to bed so early, but I’m not changing.”

#6746. Heeled Loafers Are the Shoe Trend Fashion People Are Loving Right Now

A little studious, a little unserious, and extremely easy to style.

There’s a new star shoe on the block, and it’s a little academia-core with a lot of fun. Yes, heeled loafers are the shoe fashion people are obsessing over, so you’re going to want to tap in early while you can.

We saw the shoe making its way onto several runways this past season, from Louis Vuitton and Mulberry to Diesel and Chanel. For a while now, dark academia and studious fashion have been super popular. Regular loafers are the go-to for many, whether it be wearing them to actual school or work.

The style has caught the eye of many fashion insiders, including our senior fashion editor, Jessica Neises. “The first heeled loafer I fell for this season is by Sofia Testino, an emerging NYC-based designer whose debut included the most luscious, candy-colored patent wedge loafers,” Neises says. “Other downtown brands like Vaquera have also followed suit with this shape. There’s something a little studious but still unserious about them, and the wedge is the perfect middle ground for anyone who isn’t ready to commit to a full-blown heel but doesn’t love a flat loafer either, comfortable yet still fab.”

Below, I’ve gathered some heeled loafer inspo so that you can figure out just how to style your pair.

Staying in the realm of academia, but upping the fashion, pairing your heeled loafers with a cinched blazer (and don’t forget the accessories) alongside a colorful patterned skirt is the surefire way into this trend.

As a Floridian who struggles with layering during the colder months, a good dress will always take you far. And this style works great with a more fashion-forward heeled loafer.

Similar to throwing on a dress, a good matching set will get the job done every time. Pairing a sleek leather pair of heeled loafers with this sweater set is not only stylish but super comfortable.

Aiyana Ishmael is the style editor at Cosmopolitan magazine. In her work, Aiyana focuses on the culture of fashion and how it intertwines and shapes the zeitgeist. She is an award-winning journalist from Miami, Florida, and a graduate of the historically Black university, Florida A&M. She is a 2024 Forbes 30 Under 30: Media honoree, and her debut romance novella PASSING GAME is set to release March of 2027 (831 Stories/Simon & Schuster).
